`README.md`:

```md
# Kernel and root for Meizu mBlu 21 (M416H)

Kernel sources and root images for the Meizu mBlu 21: a GKI `boot` image with a root driver built in, and an `init_boot` image that loads root as a loadable module. Two root implementations are supported, SukiSU-Ultra and KernelSU-Next.

**Start here: [docs/ROOT.md](docs/ROOT.md) — step-by-step root tutorial (unlock, write, manager, root hiding, back to stock).** What the bootloader actually enforces on this phone, and the measurements behind it, are in [docs/HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md](docs/HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md).

| | |
|---|---|
| SoC | Unisoc T606 (UMS9230), board `ums9230_6h10` |
| Stock kernel | GKI `5.15.149-android13-8-00008-gbe074b05e5af-ab12096863` (KMI `android13-5.15`) |
| Source base | `android.googlesource.com/kernel/common`, tag `android13-5.15-2024-07_r1`, commit `be074b05e5aff5eb16a848fb3aaab2f73d687cb6` |
| `boot` partition | 67,108,864 bytes, boot header v4, kernel only (stock kernel 47,172,096 bytes) |
| `init_boot` partition | 8,388,608 bytes, boot header v4, ramdisk 2,155,827 bytes in the stock image |
| `vendor_boot` | not touched by anything here; recovery and first-stage modules stay stock |

## Documentation

| Page | What it covers |
|---|---|
| [docs/ROOT.md](docs/ROOT.md) | **Root tutorial**: Linux and Windows prerequisites, bootloader unlock, download-mode writes, manager app, proving root, SUSFS setup, restore to stock, troubleshooting |
| [docs/HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md](docs/HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md) | Hardware session results: which partitions AVB verifies, what was flashed, root evidence, live partition hashes, known gaps |
| [docs/PLAY-INTEGRITY.md](docs/PLAY-INTEGRITY.md) | Play Integrity: the verdicts measured on the test phone and how they were checked |
| [root/susfs-guard-module/](root/susfs-guard-module/) | Sources of the KernelSU module that loads the SUSFS guard at boot, plus `build-module.sh` to rebuild the zip |
| [Releases](https://github.com/tickernelz/android_kernel_meizu_mblu21/releases) | `mblu21-root-20261102` holds the tested images, the SUSFS guard module and the manager APKs; `mblu21-susfs-lkm-20261102` holds the SUSFS module alone. Stock images are in [android_stock_meizu_mblu21](https://github.com/tickernelz/android_stock_meizu_mblu21/releases) |

## Status

The **LKM path is tested on hardware**: both `init_boot` images boot and give root on a real mBlu 21, and the SUSFS guard module survives a reboot (details in [docs/HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md](docs/HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md)).

The **LKM path on the unofficial PixelOS 17 ROM** ([pixelos_build_mblu21](https://github.com/tickernelz/pixelos_build_mblu21), build `20261105-1507`) **is tested on hardware** with SukiSU-Ultra `v4.3.0-r2` built from source: `ksud 4.3.0-r2`, `kernelsu` loaded, SUSFS `lkm`/`v2.3.0`/`true`, `su` gives `u:r:ksu:s0`, SELinux Enforcing, data kept (release `mblu21-pixelos-root-20261105`). That ROM ships the stock Meizu boot image, so its kernel has no KernelSU built in. The r2 images have not been tried on the stock Meizu ROM.

The **kernel path (`boot-<variant>.img`) does not boot on this phone.** On 2026-11-03 the SukiSU-Ultra image (KPM + SUSFS, `61d6e77d…`) was written to `boot_a` with everything else stock: the phone stopped at a striped Meizu logo, never enumerated on USB and left nothing in pstore, and booted again only after `boot_a` was restored from download mode. `vbmeta_a` chains `boot` to a Meizu key, so the bootloader most likely refuses the foreign signature. The images stay as CI pre-releases for other units; use the LKM path here.

## Two paths

| Path | What is flashed | Kernel changed | Root | SUSFS (root hiding) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LKM (**tested**) | `init_boot` | no | kernel module loaded by the patched ramdisk | SukiSU-Ultra: yes, via the guard module. KernelSU-Next: yes, same guard module |
| Kernel (does not boot on this phone) | `boot` | yes | driver built into the kernel | SukiSU-Ultra: yes, kernel-side SUSFS patch. KernelSU-Next: no kernel patch needed; SUSFS comes from its `susfs_guard_lkm.ko` |

LKM path status per variant:

| Variant | LKM for `android13-5.15` | Patched `init_boot` |
|---|---|---|
| KernelSU-Next | `aarch64_android13-5.15_kernelsu.ko` from release `v3.4.2` | patched on the CI host with the official `ksud-x86_64-unknown-linux-musl` (`ksud boot-patch --kmi android13-5.15`) from the stock `init_boot_a.bin` |
| SukiSU-Ultra | `lkm-aarch64-android13-5.15_kernelsu.ko` from release `v4.3.0` | patched **on the phone** with the Android `ksud` that ships in `v4.3.0`; the result is attached to the `mblu21-root-20261102` release |
| SukiSU-Ultra on PixelOS 17 | `kernelsu.ko` built from `v4.3.0-r2` (`645ef986`), byte-identical to the release asset | patched on the host with `ksud` built from `v4.3.0-r2` (run under `qemu-aarch64`) from that ROM build's `init_boot.img`; one image per ROM build, attached to `mblu21-pixelos-root-20261105` |

Pick one path and one variant. Flashing two root drivers is not a supported combination.

Why the two implementations differ here:

- SukiSU-Ultra ships SUSFS support in its `builtin` branch (its Kconfig defines `KSU_SUSFS`), but the hiding itself needs the `susfs4ksu` kernel patch, so the kernel path is built with `patches/0001-susfs-android13-5.15.patch` applied.
- KernelSU-Next keeps its SUSFS implementation in a loadable module (`susfs_guard_lkm.ko`, built from `kernel/susfs/` in its tree), so its kernel carries only the root driver and needs no source patch. That module also attaches to SukiSU-Ultra's LKM, which is why one guard module serves both variants.

## Static validation

The phone's vendor modules (113 in `vendor_dlkm`, 139 in the `vendor_boot` first-stage ramdisk) were built against the stock kernel and carry a `__versions` section: the CRC of every symbol they import. A kernel whose symbol CRCs differ from the stock kernel loads those modules only by luck; a mismatch on the symbols they use is what turns a custom kernel into a bootloop.

`scripts/module_abi.py` reads `__versions` and the `__ksymtab_*`/`__crc_*` symbols out of every stock module, stores the result as derived text in `abi/` (no vendor binaries are redistributed), and compares it against the built kernel's `Module.symvers`:

| Module set | Modules | Imported symbols | Exported symbols |
|---|---|---|---|
| `vendor_boot` first-stage ramdisk | 139 | 7,031 | 493 |
| `vendor_dlkm` | 113 | 6,621 | 540 |
| `system_dlkm` | 2 | 151 | 10 |

- A symbol imported from another stock module is skipped: that binary does not change. Across all three sets, 927 such imports point at 805 module exports, and every one of them carries the same CRC on both sides.
- `--self-test` perturbs a fifth of the kernel symbols and drops a tenth, then asserts the checker reports exactly those failures. Without it, a checker that always prints zero would look identical to a good kernel.

Result of the first green build (run 41279553995, both variants identical):

| Module set | Imports checked against vmlinux | Imports between stock modules | CRC mismatches | Missing symbols |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `vendor_boot` | 6,581 | 450 | 0 | 0 |
| `vendor_dlkm` | 6,154 | 467 | 0 | 0 |
| `system_dlkm` | 141 | 10 | 0 | 0 |

The self-test passed in all three sets. The kernel's GKI protected-symbol lists (`android/abi_gki_modules_protected`, `android/abi_gki_modules_exports`) are empty at this tag, so unsigned vendor modules are not blocked from any symbol.

Measured differences from the stock kernel, from the embedded `/proc/config.gz` of each image:

- Added: `CONFIG_KSU=y` and the root implementation's options (SukiSU-Ultra also the ten `CONFIG_KSU_SUSFS*` options).
- `CONFIG_LTO_CLANG_THIN` instead of `CONFIG_LTO_CLANG_FULL` (Kleaf `--config=fast`); symbol CRCs do not depend on it, which the gate above confirms.
- `CONFIG_LOCALVERSION_AUTO` and `CONFIG_MODULE_SCMVERSION` off: the release string is `5.15.149-maybe-dirty` instead of `5.15.149-android13-8-00008-gbe074b05e5af-ab12096863`. With `CONFIG_MODVERSIONS` the kernel ignores the release part of a module's vermagic and compares only the flags (`SMP preempt mod_unload modversions aarch64`), which match. Apps can read the different string from `uname`; SUSFS `SPOOF_UNAME` exists for that.
- Kernel size: SukiSU-Ultra 41,785,856 bytes, KernelSU-Next 41,781,760 bytes, stock 47,172,096 bytes; each `boot-<variant>.img` is padded to exactly the 67,108,864-byte partition.

CI fails the build on any mismatch or missing symbol, so `0 mismatches / 0 missing` in the job summary means the checker ran and found nothing, not that it was skipped.

What this does **not** prove: that the kernel boots, that the bootloader accepts the image, that SUSFS actually hides anything from a given app, or that the vendor modules work at runtime. Those are runtime facts and are unverified until the images run on hardware.

## Releases

Four kinds of release:

| Release | Contents |
|---|---|
| `mblu21-root-20261102` | **Tested on hardware.** `init_boot-sukisu-ultra-lkm.img` and `init_boot-kernelsu-next-lkm.img` (both boot, both give root), the SUSFS guard module (`susfs_guard_lkm.ko` plus `susfs_guard_lkm-module.zip`), both manager APKs (SukiSU-Ultra and KernelSU-Next), the patch logs, and `SHA256SUMS` |
| `mblu21-pixelos-root-20261105` | **Tested on hardware**, PixelOS 17 build `20261105-1507` only. `init_boot` patched with SukiSU-Ultra `v4.3.0-r2`, `ksud`/`kernelsu.ko`/`ksuinit` built from source, the SUSFS guard module (unchanged), the upstream r2 manager APK, the build log, `build-sukisu.sh` and `patch-init-boot.sh`, the on-phone check output, and `SHA256SUMS` |
| `mblu21-susfs-lkm-20261102` | **Tested on hardware.** The SUSFS guard KernelSU module alone (`susfs_guard_lkm.zip`, same bytes as `susfs_guard_lkm-module.zip` above) for SukiSU-Ultra in LKM mode, plus `SHA256SUMS` |
| `<variant>-<date>` (CI pre-release) | One per root variant, built by `build.yml`: `boot-<variant>.img` (the kernel path does not boot on this phone, see [Status](#status)), `init_boot-<variant>-lkm.img`, the ABI reports, `config-report.txt`, `lkm-<variant>.log` |

## Install

**The full procedure, including bootloader unlock and the download-mode writes this phone requires, is [docs/ROOT.md](docs/ROOT.md).** This section is the short form.

Prerequisites:

- Unlocked bootloader. `vbmeta` on this phone must stay Meizu-signed even when unlocked, so nothing here replaces it; `init_boot` is the one chain partition whose verification is not enforced.
- A full stock backup. `boot_a.bin` and `init_boot_a.bin` from the phone are the only reliable way back. The untouched U01005 images are also in the stock firmware release [mblu21-stock-U01005-20261031](https://github.com/tickernelz/android_stock_meizu_mblu21/releases/tag/mblu21-stock-U01005-20261031) as `boot_a.bin` and `init_boot_a.bin`.
- `adb`, and `spd_dump` from [TomKing062/CVE-2022-38694_unlock_bootloader](https://github.com/TomKing062/CVE-2022-38694_unlock_bootloader/releases) for the writes. `fastboot flash` does not work on this unit.

Verify a download first:

```sh
sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS
```

### Path B (recommended) - `init_boot` with the root module

`init_boot` carries only the generic ramdisk; the stock kernel stays in place. This is the tested path.

```sh
cd ~/tools/linux     # spd_dump + fdl1-dl.bin + fdl2-dl.bin live here
./spd_dump --wait 300 exec_addr 0x65015f08 fdl fdl1-dl.bin 0x65000800 \
    fdl fdl2-dl.bin 0x9efffe00 exec w init_boot_a ~/mblu21-root/init_boot-sukisu-ultra-lkm.img reset
```

Then install the matching manager and open it once:

| Variant | Manager |
|---|---|
| SukiSU-Ultra | `SukiSU_v4.3.0_41100-release.apk` - attached to `mblu21-root-20261102`, or from `SukiSU-Ultra/SukiSU-Ultra` release `v4.3.0` |
| KernelSU-Next | `KernelSU_Next_v3.4.2_33355-release.apk` - attached to `mblu21-root-20261102`, or from `KernelSU-Next/KernelSU-Next` release `v3.4.2` |

### Path A - kernel with the driver built in (does not boot on this phone)

```sh
./spd_dump --wait 300 exec_addr 0x65015f08 fdl fdl1-dl.bin 0x65000800 \
    fdl fdl2-dl.bin 0x9efffe00 exec w boot_a boot-sukisu-ultra.img reset
```

Expected to be rejected by the bootloader, because `vbmeta_a` chains `boot` to a Meizu key. Not measured; see [docs/HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md](docs/HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md).

### Root hiding (SUSFS)

```sh
adb push susfs_guard_lkm-module.zip /data/local/tmp/
adb shell su -c 'ksud module install /data/local/tmp/susfs_guard_lkm-module.zip'
adb reboot
```

Works on either LKM variant. Steps and verification commands are in [docs/ROOT.md](docs/ROOT.md#6-root-hiding-for-banking-apps-susfs).

## Back to stock

```sh
cd ~/tools/linux
./spd_dump --wait 300 exec_addr 0x65015f08 fdl fdl1-dl.bin 0x65000800 \
    fdl fdl2-dl.bin 0x9efffe00 exec w init_boot_a init_boot_a.bin reset
```

`init_boot_a.bin` is your backup, or the same file from the stock firmware release [mblu21-stock-U01005-20261031](https://github.com/tickernelz/android_stock_meizu_mblu21/releases/tag/mblu21-stock-U01005-20261031) (`d07159a1…`). Flashing only the partition you changed is enough. If you also replaced `boot_a`, write `boot_a.bin` (`3a78e139…`) back the same way. Userdata is untouched by both operations.

## What is not touched

- `vendor_boot`: recovery and the first-stage ramdisk stay as shipped. Recovery images live in [android_device_meizu_mblu21-recovery](https://github.com/tickernelz/android_device_meizu_mblu21-recovery), but note that this bootloader verifies `vendor_boot` against a Meizu key, so a foreign recovery does not currently load ([docs/HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md](docs/HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md)).
- `vendor_dlkm`, `system_dlkm`, `vendor`, `odm`, `dtbo`, `vbmeta*`: untouched. Nothing here needs a disabled verification chain beyond the unlocked bootloader.
- The phone itself: nothing in this repository writes to a device; images are built from sources and files in this repository.

## Provenance

| Component | Source | Pin |
|---|---|---|
| Kernel | `android.googlesource.com/kernel/common` | tag `android13-5.15-2024-07_r1` (`be074b05e5af`) via manifest branch `common-android13-5.15-2024-07` |
| SukiSU-Ultra driver (kernel path) | `github.com/SukiSU-Ultra/SukiSU-Ultra` | `builtin` at `b16e899a555e9b96aa1225f049cd7274820ca5b8` |
| KernelSU-Next driver (kernel path) | `github.com/KernelSU-Next/KernelSU-Next` | `dev` at `cefb048b069b7439fb4020d043de2d338dbc00fa` |
| SUSFS patch | `gitlab.com/simonpunk/susfs4ksu` | `gki-android13-5.15` at `3f3073a9f76247af765bfeba7908f4d3c580f2bc` |
| LKM images | flavour release assets plus a host build of `ksud` | SukiSU-Ultra `v4.3.0`, KernelSU-Next `v3.4.2` |
| PixelOS LKM image | `kernelsu.ko`, `ksud`, `ksuinit` built from source (`build-sukisu.sh` in the release) | SukiSU-Ultra `v4.3.0-r2` at `645ef9860b9b7f900bb5d1b27e224f2c311d39c0` |
| Manager APKs | unmodified upstream release assets, GPL-3.0; sources at [SukiSU-Ultra `v4.3.0`](https://github.com/SukiSU-Ultra/SukiSU-Ultra/tree/v4.3.0) and [KernelSU-Next `v3.4.2`](https://github.com/KernelSU-Next/KernelSU-Next/tree/v3.4.2) | SHA-256 equal to the upstream assets: `a928eb57…`, `774a5511…` |

`patches/0001-susfs-android13-5.15.patch` is the upstream patch set applied to `android13-5.15-2024-07_r1`. Three include-block hunks of the upstream `50_add_susfs_in_gki-android13-5.15.patch` do not apply to 5.15.149, because the file headers around them changed between sublevels; this repository carries the same two `#include <linux/susfs_def.h>` insertions and the `CL_COPY_MNT_NS` block placed by hand, and everything else is unchanged. The regenerated patch applies with zero rejects against the pinned tree, which CI re-checks on every build.

`build.yml` patches the stock `init_boot_a.bin` (the generic Android ramdisk only) downloaded from the stock firmware release [mblu21-stock-U01005-20261031](https://github.com/tickernelz/android_stock_meizu_mblu21/releases/tag/mblu21-stock-U01005-20261031) and checked against SHA-256 `d07159a1…` before use, so the LKM images rebuild reproducibly without keeping a stock image in this tree.

## Build

GitHub Actions builds everything; nothing here needs a local machine. Dispatch the workflow with a `release_tag` to publish pre-releases, or push to `main` to only run the checks.

Locally, with the pieces available:

```sh
scripts/sync_sources.sh "$HOME/gki" be074b05e5aff5eb16a848fb3aaab2f73d687cb6 8
scripts/apply_root.sh "$HOME/gki" https://github.com/SukiSU-Ultra/SukiSU-Ultra b16e899a555e9b96aa1225f049cd7274820ca5b8 patches/0001-susfs-android13-5.15.patch CONFIG_KSU=y
cd "$HOME/gki" && tools/bazel run --config=fast //common:kernel_aarch64_dist -- --dist_dir="$HOME/dist"
scripts/make_boot.sh "$HOME/dist/Image" boot-sukisu-ultra.img . 67108864
python3 scripts/module_abi.py check --imports abi/vendor_dlkm.imports.tsv --module-exports abi/vendor_boot.exports.tsv abi/vendor_dlkm.exports.tsv abi/system_dlkm.exports.tsv --symvers "$HOME/dist/kernel_aarch64_Module.symvers" --self-test
```

`scripts/bootimg.py` is a self-contained boot header v3/v4 packer; packing the stock kernel with it reproduces the stock `boot` and `init_boot` images byte for byte, which is the check that keeps it honest.

`docs/AVB-POLICY.md`:

```md
# What the mBlu 21 bootloader verifies (2026-11-02)

Every custom image we flashed on this phone died at the Meizu logo until one rule was
met. This page records the rule and the evidence, so nobody repeats the experiment.

## The rule

A `vendor_boot` / `init_boot` may carry any ramdisk, but the AVB blob stored in the
image has to stay the stock Meizu one. The bootloader compares the vbmeta public key
with the chain key recorded in the stock slot `vbmeta`. A test-key blob is rejected,
and so is a rebuilt `vbmeta` with verification disabled.

| Partition | Verified by the bootloader? | What works |
|---|---|---|
| `init_boot` | content not hashed, but the embedded blob must stay Meizu | root: Linux kernel module patched into the ramdisk (KernelSU-Next, SukiSU Ultra) |
| `vendor_boot` | same | custom recovery ramdisk: Lineage Recovery booted, OrangeFox/SHRP need their own init checks |
| `boot` | chained in `vbmeta` | do not touch; the stock GKI kernel is what boots |
| `vbmeta` | signature must be Meizu | never replace it |

## Evidence

- Stock `vendor_boot_a` embeds vbmeta `06daaf6e...` with public key `4fce36c7...`, which
  is exactly the chain key the stock `vbmeta_a` records for `vendor_boot`.
- SHRP r19 and OrangeFox r19 embed a test key (`2597c218...`) and rollback index 0; both
  stopped at the logo. So did `vbmeta` rebuilt with flags 3, unsigned and RSA-4096 signed.
- SHRP rebuilt with the stock blob inside (`12a0756a...`) booted to Android instead of
  hanging, which isolates the blob as the blocker.
- Lineage Recovery rebuilt the same way (`3f2ff23f...`, then `f484cb03...` with the
  backlight fix) reached recovery: `ro.boot.bootreason=recovery`, and recovery cleared
  the boot-control block itself.
- The root images (SukiSU Ultra 4.3.0, KernelSU-Next 3.4.2) keep the stock blob and boot
  with a ramdisk that no longer matches the recorded digest, confirming the content hash
  is not enforced on these partitions.

## Rebuilding an image with the stock blob

`tools/reseal_stock_avb.py` in `android_device_meizu_mblu21-recovery` swaps the blob and
keeps the ramdisk, DTB, cmdline and bootconfig. The reference blob is
`prebuilt/vendor_boot.avb.vbmeta` (`06daaf6e...`), taken from the stock dump.

## Two traps on this phone

- `fastboot flash` and `fastboot boot` answer `Status read failed`; write with `spd_dump`
  in Unisoc download mode or with `dd` as root.
- The bootloader uses **Volume Down** as its confirm key on the unlock warning screen.
  Pressing the physical Volume Down key inside recovery resets the phone (bootcause
  `Reboot due to sysdump finish`). Measured 2026-11-03: it happens without any root
  module in `init_boot`, with `sprd_pmic_wdt` loaded, and not for Volume Up or for a
  Volume Down event injected with `sendevent`, so it is below the recovery UI. Navigate
  with Volume Up (the menu wraps) and Power, or drive recovery over adb.
- recovery must set `/sys/class/backlight/sprd_backlight/brightness`; without it the
  screen is dark even though recovery is running.

```

`docs/HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md`:

```md
# Hardware results: 2026-11-02 session on the mBlu 21 (M416H)

Everything below was measured on the real phone (`416HF61E8DFT009433`, board `ums9230_6h10`, kernel `5.15.149-android13-8-00008-gbe074b05e5af-ab12096863`), not inferred from the images. Where an item is **not** proven, it says so.

## What the bootloader enforces

The phone was unlocked with the CVE-2022-38694 BootROM method (`ums9230_universal_unlock_UFS`), which sets the unlock flag in `miscdata` and does **not** run the usual userdata wipe: after unlocking, `/data` still held 2.9 GB, 255 packages, and FBE was active. `ro.boot.flash.locked=0`, `verifiedbootstate=orange`.

| Written | Result |
|---|---|
| stock `vbmeta_a` (flags 0, Meizu signature) | boots |
| `vbmeta` flags 3, algorithm NONE (the `fastboot --disable-verification` form) | stuck at the logo |
| `vbmeta` flags 3, **signed** RSA-4096 with the AOSP test key, no chain descriptors | stuck at the logo |
| stock `vbmeta_a` + foreign `vendor_boot_a` (SHRP) | stuck at the logo |
| stock `vbmeta_a` + stock `boot_a`/`vendor_boot_a`/`dtbo_a` + **modified `init_boot_a`** | **boots** |

So the top-level `vbmeta` must be Meizu-signed even with the bootloader unlocked, and `vendor_boot` is verified against the key in its chain descriptor (`4fce36c7…`). `init_boot` is a chain partition in `vbmeta_a` (`f1553ffc…`) but verification of it is **not** enforced in practice — a one-byte header change (`0x20`: `00`→`ff`) booted, which is what made the LKM root path possible.

Unverified in this session: no image with a foreign signature was ever written to `boot_a` while everything else stayed stock. The follow-up on 2026-11-03 did write one (a SukiSU-Ultra kernel) and the phone stopped at the logo, so the kernel path does not boot on this phone; see the README Status.

## Write path

`fastboot flash` and `fastboot boot` are read-only on this unit: they answer `Sending … OKAY` and then fail at `Writing` (`Status read failed (No such device)`) or never start. Everything that was written went through `spd_dump` in Unisoc download mode (USB `1782:4d00`), entering it with **Volume Up + plug in** (Volume Down gave only the charging screen). Each `spd_dump` session drops the link on reset, so every write needs a fresh hold-and-plug window.

## Root, both variants, proven

| Variant | Image written to `init_boot_a` | Boot | Root |
|---|---|---|---|
| SukiSU-Ultra 4.3.0 | `init_boot-sukisu-ultra-lkm.img` `cd060b9ede002187c4b90a9f76f16247f39ff78ec1995dad229465d0ede6a5fa` | boots | `su -c id` → `uid=0`, ctx `u:r:ksu:s0`; `ksud 4.3.0 (uapi: 6)`; manager `com.sukisu.ultra` v4.3.0 |
| KernelSU-Next 3.4.2 | `init_boot-kernelsu-next-lkm.img` `3ed94999b93d60c2e01202ef78d7522dc42f045b766dd0de1bc6c83c07b92592` | boots | `su -c id` → `uid=0`, ctx `u:r:ksu:s0`; `ksud 3.4.2` |

In both cases `getenforce` stayed **Enforcing**, the kernel stayed the stock `5.15.149` build, and `boot`, `vendor_boot`, `dtbo`, `vbmeta` were left at their stock Meizu content. Live partition hashes at the end of the session: `boot_a` `3a78e139…` (= stock), `init_boot_a` `cd060b9e…` (patched), `vendor_boot_a` `c053086c…` (= stock), `vbmeta_a` `83789a72…` (= stock), `dtbo_a` `71e5cae7…` (= stock).

### How the SukiSU-Ultra image was produced

SukiSU-Ultra v4.3.0 publishes `ksud` for Android only, so the patch ran **on the phone**, not on a build host:

```sh
/data/adb/ksud boot-patch -b /data/local/tmp/init_boot.stock.img \
  -m lkm-aarch64-android13-5.15_kernelsu.ko \
  --kmi android13-5.15 --partition init_boot
```

The output was pulled and written to `init_boot_a` through download mode. The same command with KernelSU-Next's `ksud` and LKM reproduces the other image, but that one is also built in CI (`scripts/patch_init_boot.sh`) because KernelSU-Next ships a host-side `ksud`.

## SUSFS (root hiding) on the LKM path

`susfs_guard_lkm.ko` `024aa0de96b9e2d45c7f9a086189d72f95d51028fec6621b48e5f548cd39dc26` (v2.3.0-gki, 601 624 B) is the KernelSU-Next SUSFS module. It attaches to **SukiSU-Ultra** as well, because it resolves the root driver's symbols by name at load time (`ksu_uid_should_umount`, `ksu_patch_text`, `add_try_umount` are all present in SukiSU-Ultra 4.3.0's `kernelsu.ko`) instead of linking against a fixed CRC.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `ksud susfs implementation` / `version` / `variant` / `status` | `lkm` / `v2.3.0` / `GKI` / `true` |
| Manager home screen | `SuSFS Version v2.3.0 (Tracepoint Syscall Redirect)` |
| Supercall transport | kprobe on the `reboot` syscall — dmesg `susfs supercall: armed (reboot ABI, version v2.3.0)` |
| `/proc/susfs_*` | `path`, `kstat`, `opath`, `open_redirect`, `enable_log`, `avc_spoof`, `hide_modules`, `hide_mounts` |
| `ksud susfs set-uname` | `uname -r`/`uname -v` from a non-root shell showed the spoofed strings, then the stock strings were restored |
| `lsmod` | empty by design; the module removes itself from `/proc/modules` for every caller, use `/sys/module/susfs_guard_lkm` |

The module's own vermagic (`5.15.202-android13-5.15.202_r00-dirty`) differs from the running kernel (`5.15.149-android13-8-…`), and it still loads: `ksud insmod` loads with kallsyms access and skips the module version check, and the guard matches symbols by name rather than by CRC.

### Boot persistence

A bare `insmod` does not survive a reboot, so the module ships as a KernelSU module (`susfs_guard_lkm-module.zip`, `55740001f1133f067b324a316c779fe8907275d120dfc94cb9d8cef359ac9f66`): `module.prop` plus `post-fs-data.sh` and `service.sh`, both of which call `/data/adb/ksud insmod /data/adb/modules/susfs_guard_lkm/susfs_guard_lkm.ko` and return early when `/sys/module/susfs_guard_lkm` already exists.

After `adb reboot`, `/data/adb/ksu/log/susfs_guard.log` records `2026-11-02 17:18:27 loading …` and `17:18:29 rc=0 present=yes`; root kept working, SELinux stayed Enforcing, and `init_boot` was not touched again.

`ksud susfs module install` is a different thing: it writes `susfs_manager` (v4.0.0), a settings module that re-applies saved SUSFS configuration at boot. It neither ships nor loads an LKM.

## Known gaps

- **Kernel path (`boot-<variant>.img`) does not boot** (measured 2026-11-03): `boot-sukisu-ultra.img` `61d6e77d…` in `boot_a`, everything else stock, gives a striped logo, no USB enumeration and an empty pstore. Restoring the stock `boot_a` from download mode brings the phone back. The chain descriptor for `boot` in `vbmeta_a` points at Meizu's key `d46eb3be…`.
- **`vendor_boot` is not enforced after all** (corrected 2026-11-03): the LineageOS 24.0 `vendor_boot` signed with the AOSP test key boots daily, and OrangeFox fox_16.0 in `vendor_boot_a` reaches recovery with root adb. The earlier SHRP failure came from the first-stage fstab `avb` flags, not from AVB on `vendor_boot`. OrangeFox did not decrypt FBE (`twrp decrypt` hung).
- **SUSFS does not hide the manager app and does not pass Play Integrity.** Apps that call the Integrity API need a separate PIF-style module.
- **ROOT via LKM only.** Nothing here proves a rebuilt kernel boots, even though the ABI gate shows zero CRC mismatches.

```

`docs/PLAY-INTEGRITY.md`:

```md
# Play Integrity on the mBlu 21

Every number below was measured on this phone, Meizu mBlu 21 (M416H), Android 17
(API 37) build `CP2A.260605.016`, bootloader unlocked, SukiSU-Ultra 4.3.0-r2 (LKM),
SUSFS guard v2.3.0-gki, NeoZygisk v2.4. The verdict comes from Simple Play Integrity
Checker reading the raw token from Play, not from a summary screen.

## What Google requires

Google's June 2025 verdict rework (developer.android.com/google/play/integrity/improvements,
summarised by bayton.org on 2026-09-14) states the two labels this phone cannot reach:

* `MEETS_DEVICE_INTEGRITY` requires hardware-backed evidence that the bootloader is **locked**
  and that the running OS is a **certified OEM image**.
* `MEETS_STRONG_INTEGRITY` requires that plus a security patch level from the last year on every
  partition, vendor included.
* `MEETS_BASIC_INTEGRITY` is the one label allowed on an unlocked, rooted device, as long as key
  attestation is present at all.

The bootloader of this unit cannot be locked again with its own key: `vbmeta_a` chains to the
Meizu key `d46eb3be` and `vendor_boot` to `4fce36c7`, and the 2026-11-03 attempt stopped at the
boot logo. The only way to produce a verifying "locked" claim would be an attestation chain signed
by an unrevoked Google keybox, which is another device's private key material; no such keybox is
carried by this project and none was fetched.

## Result

| Configuration | Keybox in use | Keystore interposer | Device verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| Before any work: combined third-party drop-in (`AlwaysStrong [Inject]` v1.0.5) | `evokerrkey44.xml`, ecdsa only | TEESimulator | `MEETS_BASIC_INTEGRITY` |
| `mblu21-integrity` v1.0.0, `keybox_mode=existing` | `evokerrkey44.xml`, ecdsa only | Tricky Store 1.4.1 | `MEETS_BASIC_INTEGRITY` on 3 consecutive boots |
| `mblu21-integrity` v1.0.0, `keybox_mode=software` | Tricky Store's software keybox, ecdsa + rsa | Tricky Store 1.4.1 | `MEETS_BASIC_INTEGRITY` on 2 consecutive boots |
| `AlwaysStrong` restored as a cross-check | Tricky Store's software keybox, ecdsa + rsa | TEESimulator | `UNEVALUATED` twice |
| `mblu21-integrity` v1.0.0, `keybox_mode=software` (test only; shipped default is `auto`) | Tricky Store's software keybox, ecdsa + rsa | Tricky Store 1.4.1 | `MEETS_BASIC_INTEGRITY` |

`MEETS_DEVICE_INTEGRITY` was not reached in any configuration, and
`MEETS_STRONG_INTEGRITY` never appeared at all.

## Why DEVICE is unreachable here

Both interposers were tested with the same three inputs — a certified Pixel fingerprint,
a security patch level that matches it, and Play Services and Play Store in the target
list — and the verdict stayed at `MEETS_BASIC_INTEGRITY` or dropped to `UNEVALUATED`.

Play derives the device verdict from a key attestation record it asks for itself. On this
phone that record is genuine: KeyAttestation (`io.github.vvb2060.keyattestation`, a target
package) reports the real chain — `SERIALNUMBER=f92009e853b6b045` (Google's hardware
attestation root), two TEE intermediates, and a fresh leaf — with
`Bootloader is unlocked` in the attestation extension. So the bootloader state Play sees is
the hardware truth, and a hardware-backed claim of a locked bootloader needs a keybox whose
chain Google signed and whose keys have not been revoked. The phone has no such keybox.

The two keyboxes that were available behave differently and both fail:

* `evokerrkey44.xml` carries one `ecdsa` key and a four-certificate chain and no `rsa`
  key. Play asks for both, logs `W/KeyProviderManager: Incomplete keybox provided by
  overlays`, and falls back to the real TEE record.
* The software keybox that ships inside the Tricky Store release (`DeviceID="sw"`) does
  carry `ecdsa` and `rsa`, but its chain is self-signed, so it cannot stand in for a
  Google-signed one. With it in place Play returned `UNEVALUATED` instead of a verdict.

`MEETS_STRONG_INTEGRITY` additionally requires hardware-backed proof, so it is out of
reach for the same reason and was never pursued.

## Tricky Store 1.4.1 does not inject on this ROM

The module runs, but it has no effect here, and that is measured, not assumed:

* `service.sh` starts at boot (`/data/adb/tricky_store/integrity.log` records
  `service.sh start` → `pif service stage done` → `starting the TrickyStore daemon loop`),
  and the daemon loop runs: 11 `MainKt` launches in about two minutes, each one logging
  `Load /data/adb/modules/playintegrityfix/libtricky_store.so using system ns: ok` and then
  exiting 0.
* Nothing is injected: the target app's attestation stays genuine (see above), no
  `/data/adb/tricky_store/key_db` is ever created, and `keystore2` does not map the library.

Reading `/proc/<pid>/maps` cannot be used as evidence on this phone because
`CONFIG_KSU_SUSFS_SUS_MAP` hides mappings; the conclusion above comes from the functional
KeyAttestation result instead.

## What the ROM must not write

`resetprop -n -p` creates `/data/property/persistent_properties` without an explicit mode,
and under init's umask 0 the file lands at `0666`. init then refuses it —
`init: Could not load single persistent property file ... Skipping insecure file` — and
every persistent property is lost for that boot, including `persist.sys.usb.config=adb`, so
adbd never comes up on its own.

The module repairs this on every boot (`bin/fix-persist.sh`, called from
`post-fs-data.sh` after the Play Integrity Fork prop stage): it restores the file to
`0600` and re-arms `persist.sys.usb.config=adb`. `tests/run.sh` covers it with a negative
control (0666 in, 0600 out). Measured after the fix: a reboot with the phone untouched
brought `adb devices` back online 38 s after `adb reboot`, with no `keyguardGoingAway`
or Settings activity in logcat before it.

## Sustained BASIC, and the other invariants

The shipped configuration (`keybox_mode=auto`, so an existing keybox is kept and the bundled one is only
installed when there is none, `auto_keybox=0`, `ts_daemon=0`, target list of four packages,
`fix-persist.sh` active) produced, boot after boot,
`deviceRecognitionVerdict: ["MEETS_BASIC_INTEGRITY"]` with
`appRecognitionVerdict: PLAY_RECOGNIZED` and `appLicensingVerdict: LICENSED` — including three
consecutive boots on the earlier keybox configuration. `bin/boot-verdict.sh` now repeats that
measurement 60 s after every boot and appends the parsed result to
`/data/adb/tricky_store/verdicts.jsonl`, so each boot documents itself instead of relying on a
person to press a button; `bin/integrity.sh verdicts 5` prints the tail.

`bin/integrity.sh doctor` also checks the local hiding invariants that matter more than a verdict
here: SUSFS active, no `/system/bin/su` in the default namespace, `persistent_properties` at
`0600`, and `persist.sys.usb.config` still carrying `adb`.

`su_compat` was on (`ksud feature list` → `[ENABLED] su_compat (ID=0)`), which is why a 6.1 MB
`/system/bin/su` existed; `ksud feature set su_compat 0` removes it, and root over adb then goes
through adbd itself (`adb shell id` → `uid=0(root) ... context=u:r:ksu:s0`). With `su_compat` off,
`adb shell su -c ...` no longer exists, so every command in this document is meant to be run as
`adb shell <command>` on a root adbd.

## CPU cost of the keystore daemon

Tricky Store's daemon exits after a second or two on this ROM, and the upstream `service.sh` respawns it
every two seconds. Measured with `/proc/<loop pid>/stat` fields 16 and 17 over a 60.13 s window:
`19189 17319` → `20064 18152`, i.e. 1708 ticks = **0.284 core, continuously**, for a daemon that does
not modify the attestation here. `ts_daemon` is therefore `0` by default and the loop is not started;
with the loop stopped the same window measures **0 ticks**, and a verdict taken afterwards still returns
`MEETS_BASIC_INTEGRITY`. Setting `ts_daemon=1` starts the loop with a backoff that stops it after five
runs shorter than 10 s and writes that to `integrity.log`.

## How the verdict was read

```sh
adb shell sh /data/adb/modules/playintegrityfix/bin/integrity.sh verdict
```

That wakes the phone, resets Simple Play Integrity Checker, taps its request button and
prints the raw `System.out` JSON from logcat; `tools/measure-spic.sh` runs the same thing
from the host over adb.

```

`docs/ROOT.md`:

```md
# Root tutorial — Meizu mBlu 21 (M416H), step by step

The unlock and the `init_boot` writes below were run on this phone from Fedora on 2026-11-02; the same download-mode tooling had already read every partition out of the phone from Windows, which is where the 44-file dump came from. Both hosts use the same `spd_dump` with the same arguments, and the page gives the command for each. What was measured, with the partition hashes, is in [HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md](HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md).

**What this does and does not touch.** Only `init_boot_a` is written. `boot`, `vendor_boot`, `dtbo` and `vbmeta` keep their Meizu content, which is the whole trick: this bootloader rejects any foreign `vbmeta` and any foreign `vendor_boot`, but it does not enforce verification of `init_boot`, so a root module loaded from that ramdisk boots fine.

**Cost.** Unlocking with the CVE-2022-38694 method sets a flag in `miscdata`; it does **not** wipe userdata. That was measured: after unlocking, `/data` still held 2.9 GB, 255 packages, and FBE was still active. Formatting is only needed if you later flash a different Android system.

---

## 0. Prepare the host

### Linux host

```sh
sudo dnf install -y android-tools openssl libusb1-devel gcc make
```

Download-mode USB (`1782:4d00`) needs a udev rule so `spd_dump` works without root:

```sh
echo 'SUBSYSTEM=="usb", ATTR{idVendor}=="1782", ATTR{idProduct}=="4d00", TAG+="uaccess"' \
  | sudo tee /etc/udev/rules.d/51-unisoc-download.rules
sudo udevadm control --reload
```

Build the unlock and write tools from [TomKing062/CVE-2022-38694_unlock_bootloader](https://github.com/TomKing062/CVE-2022-38694_unlock_bootloader/releases): `spd_dump`, `gen_spl-unlock`, `chsize`. Keep them in one working directory, `~/mblu21-tools`, together with `fdl1-dl.bin`, `fdl2-dl.bin`, `fdl2-cboot.bin`, `misc-wipe.bin`, `spl-unlock.bin` and `custom_exec_no_verify_65015f08.bin` from the same release. Every `spd_dump` command below runs from that directory.

### Back up first (both hosts)

Two partitions decide whether you can undo everything: `init_boot_a` (what root replaces) and `boot_a`. Before root you have no `su`, so they come out through download mode:

```sh
cd ~/mblu21-tools
./spd_dump --wait 300 exec_addr 0x65015f08 fdl fdl1-dl.bin 0x65000800 fdl fdl2-dl.bin 0x9efffe00 exec \
  r init_boot_a r boot_a r vendor_boot_a r vbmeta_a reset
```

That leaves `init_boot_a.bin` (8 388 608 B) and `boot_a.bin` (67 108 864 B) in the working directory. **Keep them.** They are the only reliable way back to stock.

### Windows host

Everything below works on Windows 10/11 with the same CVE-2022-38694 release, which ships `spd_dump.exe`, `gen_spl-unlock.exe` and `chsize.exe`.

1. **Install the Unisoc USB driver.** From the release, run `SPD_Driver_R4.20.4201/Win10/DPInst64.exe` (or `Win7`/`Win8` for those systems) and accept the driver prompt. Without it, Windows never binds the download-mode device and `spd_dump.exe` reports `find port failed` forever.
2. **Get the tools.** Extract the same `ums9230_universal_unlock_UFS` package into a short path such as `C:\mblu21\tools`; the tools write their dumps and the patched image into the current directory, so it has to be writable. That folder must contain `spd_dump.exe`, `gen_spl-unlock.exe`, `chsize.exe`, `fdl1-dl.bin`, `fdl2-dl.bin`, `fdl2-cboot.bin`, `misc-wipe.bin`, `spl-unlock.bin` and `custom_exec_no_verify_65015f08.bin`.
3. **Get adb.** Platform-tools from Google (`adb.exe`, `fastboot.exe`); only `adb` is needed for the checks and the manager install.
4. **Open a Command Prompt in that folder** (`cd /d C:\mblu21\tools`) and run the commands from this page without the `./` prefix: `spd_dump --wait 300 ...`.

The release also ships `unlock_autopatch_9230.bat`, which runs the whole unlock sequence with pauses. It is fine for the unlock step, but it is not the whole job: patching `init_boot_a` and writing it still needs the commands below.

The Windows tools need the phone in the same download mode (§1), and the same rule applies: after every `spd_dump.exe` run the phone leaves download mode, so re-enter it for the next write. `fastboot.exe flash` fails on this unit exactly as it does on Linux (`Status read failed`), so it is not used anywhere in this tutorial.


---

## 1. Enter download mode

This is the step people get wrong, so read it once:

1. Unplug USB and power the phone **off**.
2. Hold **Volume Up**.
3. Plug USB in while still holding it.
4. Keep holding for about ten seconds, then release. The screen stays black.

Check from the host:

```sh
lsusb | grep 1782     # must print 1782:4d00
```

On this unit **Volume Down only reached the charging screen**; Volume Up is the combination that reaches `1782:4d00`. If the phone boots Android instead, repeat from step 1. On Windows the check is **Device Manager > Ports**, where the phone must appear as `SPRD U2S Diag (COM<n>)`; an unknown device there means the Unisoc driver from the Windows host section is missing. Every `spd_dump` run drops the USB link when it finishes, so download mode is entered freshly for every write — that is normal, not a failure.

---

## 2. Unlock the bootloader

The ROM in the phone does not route through `fastboot flashing unlock`; the Unisoc BootROM is used instead (CVE-2022-38694). Run these from the tools directory, entering download mode before each line that opens a session.

The commands below are written for Linux. On Windows they are identical without the `./` prefix, and the supplied `unlock_autopatch_9230.bat` runs this whole section with pauses between the steps, so it is the easier route there.

Read the current SPL and U-Boot, and resign the SPL with the unlock patch:

```sh
./spd_dump --wait 300 exec_addr 0x65015f08 fdl fdl1-dl.bin 0x65000800 \
    fdl fdl2-dl.bin 0x9efffe00 exec r splloader r uboot e splloader e splloader_bak reset
./gen_spl-unlock splloader.bin
mv splloader.bin u-boot-spl-16k-sign.bin
./chsize uboot.bin
mv uboot.bin uboot_bak.bin
```

Boot the unlock payload through U-Boot:

```sh
./spd_dump --wait 300 exec_addr 0x65015f08 fdl fdl1-dl.bin 0x65000800 \
    fdl fdl2-dl.bin 0x9efffe00 exec w uboot fdl2-cboot.bin reset
sleep 10
./spd_dump exec_addr 0x65015f08 fdl spl-unlock.bin 0x65000800
```

Verify the flag, then restore the stock SPL and U-Boot and clear `misc`:

```sh
./spd_dump exec_addr 0x65015f08 fdl fdl1-dl.bin 0x65000800 fdl fdl2-dl.bin 0x9efffe00 exec \
    verbose 2 read_part miscdata 8192 64 m.bin reset
xxd m.bin | head
```

- 64 zero bytes → still locked. Run the `w uboot fdl2-cboot.bin` and `fdl spl-unlock.bin` lines again.
- A device string plus two hashes → unlocked.

```sh
./spd_dump exec_addr 0x65015f08 fdl fdl1-dl.bin 0x65000800 fdl fdl2-dl.bin 0x9efffe00 exec \
    r boot w splloader u-boot-spl-16k-sign.bin w uboot uboot_bak.bin w misc misc-wipe.bin reset
```

Power the phone on normally: it boots the stock Android 14 system, now with `ro.boot.flash.locked=0` and `verifiedbootstate=orange` ("LOCK FLAG IS: UNLOCK" on the splash).

**The unlock does not erase your data.** Measured on this phone: after unlocking, `/data` still held 2.9 GB, 255 packages were installed, and FBE was still active. The CVE-2022-38694 method only flips the flag in `miscdata`; it never runs the wipe that `fastboot flashing unlock` would. Formatting becomes necessary only when a different Android system is installed later.

---

## 3. Pick one root implementation

Both were booted on this phone; pick one, not both. They replace the same file (`init_boot_a`), so switching later is just flashing the other image.

| | SukiSU-Ultra 4.3.0 | KernelSU-Next 3.4.2 |
|---|---|---|
| Image | `init_boot-sukisu-ultra-lkm.img` | `init_boot-kernelsu-next-lkm.img` |
| SUSFS (root hiding) | yes, by installing the guard module (§6) | yes, by installing the guard module (§6) |
| Manager app | `SukiSU_v4.3.0_41100-release.apk` | `KernelSU_Next_v3.4.2_33355-release.apk` |
| Manager in the release | yes (`a928eb57…`) | yes (`774a5511…`) |
| Extra kernel extensions (KPM, CPU spoof) | yes | no |

SukiSU-Ultra is the one this phone ended up running. Take the image from the release page (`mblu21-root-20261102`) and check it:

```sh
mkdir -p ~/mblu21-root && cd ~/mblu21-root
gh release download mblu21-root-20261102 -R tickernelz/android_kernel_meizu_mblu21
sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS
```

The two images are:

| File | sha256 |
|---|---|
| `init_boot-sukisu-ultra-lkm.img` | `cd060b9ede002187c4b90a9f76f16247f39ff78ec1995dad229465d0ede6a5fa` |
| `init_boot-kernelsu-next-lkm.img` | `3ed94999b93d60c2e01202ef78d7522dc42f045b766dd0de1bc6c83c07b92592` |

---

## 4. Write the image through download mode

`fastboot flash` does **not** work on this unit: it answers `Sending … OKAY` and then fails at `Writing` with `Status read failed (No such device)`. Every write goes through `spd_dump`.

Enter download mode (§1), then:

```sh
cd ~/mblu21-tools
D=~/mblu21-root
./spd_dump --wait 300 exec_addr 0x65015f08 fdl fdl1-dl.bin 0x65000800 \
    fdl fdl2-dl.bin 0x9efffe00 exec w init_boot_a $D/init_boot-sukisu-ultra-lkm.img reset
```

Windows, from the tools folder with the image copied next to the tools (`^` continues a line in `cmd.exe`):

```bat
spd_dump --wait 300 exec_addr 0x65015f08 fdl fdl1-dl.bin 0x65000800 ^
    fdl fdl2-dl.bin 0x9efffe00 exec w init_boot_a init_boot-sukisu-ultra-lkm.img reset
```

Success looks like `Write Part Done` followed by `rc=0`. If the run ends with `LIBUSB_ERROR_IO` or `find port failed`, the phone left download mode before finishing: enter it again and rerun the same command. Nothing is half-written in that case — the partition keeps its previous content.

Only `init_boot_a` is written. If the phone ever boots from slot `_b`, write `init_boot_b` with the same image.

---

## 5. Boot and install the manager

Unplug, press Power, wait for Android to finish booting. Then:

```sh
adb install ~/mblu21-root/SukiSU_v4.3.0_41100-release.apk
```

On Windows use `adb.exe` with the folder you actually downloaded to, for example `adb install C:\mblu21\root\SukiSU_v4.3.0_41100-release.apk`. Every `adb shell` command in this tutorial works the same there as long as the single quotes are kept: `cmd.exe` passes them through, and the shell on the phone is what needs them.

Open the app once. It should show the kernel as `LKM`, the version as `Working`, and the manager version `v4.3.0`. Grant root to the apps you want.

### Prove root from the host

```sh
adb shell su -c 'id'
```

Expected:

```
uid=0(root) gid=0(root) ... context=u:r:ksu:s0
```

More checks:

```sh
adb shell su -c '/data/adb/ksud -V'        # ksud 4.3.0 (uapi: 6)
adb shell su -c 'getenforce'               # Enforcing
adb shell su -c 'uname -r'                 # 5.15.149-android13-8-00008-gbe074b05e5af-ab12096863
```

If `su` hangs, open the manager app: the first `su` call asks for permission and the prompt is on the phone screen, not in the terminal.

SELinux stays **Enforcing** with the LKM path — there is no reason to set permissive.

---

## 6. Root hiding for banking apps (SUSFS)

Root alone is detected by most banking apps. SUSFS hides the interesting parts: mount points, `/proc` entries, module list, kernel version, and the root driver's own symbols.

Install the guard module; it is included in the same release as `susfs_guard_lkm-module.zip`:

```sh
adb push susfs_guard_lkm-module.zip /data/local/tmp/
adb shell su -c 'ksud module install /data/local/tmp/susfs_guard_lkm-module.zip'
adb reboot
```

After the reboot:

```sh
adb shell su -c '/data/adb/ksud susfs implementation'   # lkm
adb shell su -c '/data/adb/ksud susfs version'          # v2.3.0
adb shell su -c '/data/adb/ksud susfs variant'          # GKI
adb shell su -c '/data/adb/ksud susfs status'           # true
adb shell su -c 'cat /data/adb/ksu/log/susfs_guard.log'
```

The log must end with a line containing `rc=0 present=yes` for the boot that just happened.

```sh
adb shell su -c 'ksud susfs set-uname 5.15.149-android13-8-00008-gbe074b05e5af-ab12096863 "#1 SMP PREEMPT Tue Jul 16 15:36:08 UTC 2024"'
```

`set-uname` is the one check that proves the whole chain end to end: from a **non-root** shell, `uname -r` then reports the string you set. Put the stock pair back when you are done experimenting.

Two things SUSFS does not fix, so do not chase them in this repo:

- The manager app itself stays installed and visible. Hiding it is a manager-side feature.
- Play Integrity attestation still fails; apps that call the Integrity API need a PIF-style module.

---

## 7. Go back to stock

```sh
cd ~/mblu21-tools
./spd_dump --wait 300 exec_addr 0x65015f08 fdl fdl1-dl.bin 0x65000800 \
    fdl fdl2-dl.bin 0x9efffe00 exec w init_boot_a ~/mblu21-backup/init_boot_a.bin reset
```

Windows: the same command with `spd_dump` instead of `./spd_dump` and `init_boot_a.bin` in the tools folder.

The same untouched images are in the stock firmware release [mblu21-stock-U01005-20261031](https://github.com/tickernelz/android_stock_meizu_mblu21/releases/tag/mblu21-stock-U01005-20261031) as `init_boot_a.bin` (`d07159a1…`) and `boot_a.bin` (`3a78e139…`). Boot once and check `adb shell su -c id` fails — that is the root gone. Userdata is untouched by this operation.

Leaving the bootloader unlocked does not by itself break apps; it only prints the orange warning on the splash screen.

---

## 8. Verify the whole install in one go

One host command proves root, the manager, SELinux and SUSFS:

```sh
adb shell su -c 'id; /data/adb/ksud -V; getenforce; /data/adb/ksud susfs implementation'
```

Expected: `uid=0(root) ... context=u:r:ksu:s0`, a `ksud` version, `Enforcing`, and `lkm` once the guard module from §6 is installed.

Compare the untouched partitions against the stock hashes; only `init_boot_a` may differ:

```sh
for p in boot_a vendor_boot_a vbmeta_a dtbo_a; do
  printf "%s " $p
  adb shell su -c "dd if=/dev/block/by-name/$p bs=1M 2>/dev/null | sha256sum"
done
```

On Windows run the same `adb shell` line once per partition and compare the hash by eye (`cmd.exe` has no `for` loop worth writing).

Stock values measured on the reference phone: `boot_a` `3a78e1394a5c3c52252384c7a136ae3c6c4fd758f0ba4a781c91813b1352f6da`, `vendor_boot_a` `c053086cefb9845e8b604224719f71d1b2503e46fee528ba1cd7864a9ea0ed29`, `vbmeta_a` `83789a72825563cc6fe6e93a5658f2f06a962e09c6f1884d46e15ffb7b7607aa`, `dtbo_a` `71e5cae7dfabc341e4d39d7f15dadb72aec8636e68a33654b99619532936874e`. A different value means something other than `init_boot_a` was written.

## 9. When something goes wrong

| Symptom | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `lsusb` never shows `1782:4d00` | wrong key or timing | power off, hold **Volume Up**, plug in, hold ten seconds |
| Windows: no `SPRD U2S Diag (COM<n>)` appears | Unisoc driver missing, or the port is an unknown device | install `SPD_Driver_R4.20.4201/Win10/DPInst64.exe`, replug |
| Windows: `spd_dump.exe` prints `find port failed` in a loop | phone not in download mode, or a path with spaces | re-enter download mode, move the tools to `C:\mblu21\tools` |
| `fastboot flash` ends with `Status read failed` | fastboot is read-only here | use `spd_dump` (§4) |
| `LIBUSB_ERROR_IO` mid-write | phone left download mode | re-enter download mode, rerun the same command |
| stuck on the Meizu logo | the image written to `init_boot_a` is bad, or `vbmeta`/`vendor_boot` was replaced | enter download mode, write stock `init_boot_a`, and stock `vbmeta_a`/`vendor_boot_a` if you changed them |
| boots, but no `su` | manager app not installed, or root not granted yet | install the manager (§5), open it, grant |
| `ksud` not found | the LKM image was never written, or the patched image was overwritten by a stock restore | reflash the patched `init_boot_a` |
| SUSFS not loaded after reboot | module missing or `ksud insmod` failed | `cat /data/adb/ksu/log/susfs_guard.log`, reinstall §6 |
| banking app still refuses | manager visible or Play Integrity failing | §6 notes: not covered here |

A phone that will not boot is not a brick: the BootROM is reachable on every power-up, so download mode plus a stock image always brings it back.

## 10. PixelOS 17 ROM: SukiSU-Ultra v4.3.0-r2

Sections 1-9 are for the stock Meizu ROM and its `v4.3.0` images. On the unofficial PixelOS 17 ROM ([pixelos_build_mblu21](https://github.com/tickernelz/pixelos_build_mblu21)) root uses the same LKM path with SukiSU-Ultra `v4.3.0-r2` built from source, release `mblu21-pixelos-root-20261105`. That ROM ships the stock Meizu boot image, so the kernel is unchanged and has no KernelSU built in.

| | Stock ROM (§3) | PixelOS 17 build `20261105-1507` |
|---|---|---|
| SukiSU-Ultra | `v4.3.0` | `v4.3.0-r2` (`645ef986`) |
| Image | `init_boot-sukisu-ultra-lkm.img` | `init_boot-PixelOS_mblu21-17.0-20261105-1507-sukisu-v4.3.0-r2.img` (`da2cac6d…`) |
| Manager | `SukiSU_v4.3.0_41100-release.apk` | `SukiSU_v4.3.0-r2_41150-release.apk` |
| SUSFS guard | `susfs_guard_lkm` `v2.3.0` | same module, same bytes |

A patched `init_boot` fits one ROM build only. For another PixelOS build, patch that build's `init_boot.img` with `patch-init-boot.sh` from the release; installing a ROM update without a repatched `init_boot` drops root.

Install from a phone that is already rooted (commands in the release notes), or write the image to the active slot (`init_boot_a` or `init_boot_b`, see `getprop ro.boot.slot_suffix`) with `spd_dump` as in §4. Then check:

```sh
adb shell su -c '/data/adb/ksud -V'              # ksud 4.3.0-r2 (uapi: 6)
adb shell su -c 'grep ^kernelsu /proc/modules'   # kernelsu ... Live
adb shell su -c '/data/adb/ksud susfs status'    # true
adb shell su -c 'getenforce'                     # Enforcing
```

Those outputs were measured on the phone on 2026-11-05 after installing from a rooted phone with `dd`. The `spd_dump` write of this image and a first install on an unrooted PixelOS phone were not tested.

---

## Related reading

- [HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md](HARDWARE-2026-11-02.md) — what the bootloader enforces, what was measured, live partition hashes.
- [../README.md](../README.md) — how the images are built, ABI gate, provenance.
- [android_device_meizu_mblu21-recovery](https://github.com/tickernelz/android_device_meizu_mblu21-recovery) — recovery images (note: `vendor_boot` is AVB-enforced on this phone, so recovery does not currently load).

```

`keys/README.md`:

```md
# keys

`testkey_rsa4096.pem` is the **public AOSP test key** from [`external/avb/test/data/testkey_rsa4096.pem`](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/external/avb/+/refs/heads/main/test/data/testkey_rsa4096.pem), byte-identical (SHA-256 `6a224754880a57ab9cbd308267cd157d94cf05a1c8cb851aec4090e045d24121`). It is **not a release key**: anyone can sign with it, and it only gives `boot-<variant>.img` a well-formed AVB footer. The phone does not trust it; see [docs/AVB-POLICY.md](../docs/AVB-POLICY.md).

```

+#ifdef CONFIG_KSU_SUSFS_SUS_MOUNT
+/* A copy of alloc_vfsmnt() but allocates the fake mnt_id for mounts
+ * that are unshared by ksu process
+ */
+static struct mount *susfs_alloc_unshare_ksu_vfsmnt(const char *name, int old_mnt_id)
+{
+	struct mount *mnt = kmem_cache_zalloc(mnt_cache, GFP_KERNEL);
+
+	if (mnt) {
+		mnt->mnt_id = old_mnt_id;
+
+		if (name) {
+			mnt->mnt_devname = kstrdup_const(name,
+							 GFP_KERNEL_ACCOUNT);
+			if (!mnt->mnt_devname)
+				goto out_free_cache;
+		}
+
+#ifdef CONFIG_SMP
+		mnt->mnt_pcp = alloc_percpu(struct mnt_pcp);
+		if (!mnt->mnt_pcp)
+			goto out_free_devname;
+
+		this_cpu_add(mnt->mnt_pcp->mnt_count, 1);
+#else
+		mnt->mnt_count = 1;
+		mnt->mnt_writers = 0;
+#endif
+
+		INIT_HLIST_NODE(&mnt->mnt_hash);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_child);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_mounts);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_list);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_expire);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_share);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_slave_list);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_slave);
+		INIT_HLIST_NODE(&mnt->mnt_mp_list);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_umounting);
+		INIT_HLIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_stuck_children);
+		mnt->mnt.mnt_userns = &init_user_ns;
+	}
+	return mnt;
+
+#ifdef CONFIG_SMP
+out_free_devname:
+	kfree_const(mnt->mnt_devname);
+#endif
+out_free_cache:
+	kmem_cache_free(mnt_cache, mnt);
+	return NULL;
+}

+/* A copy of alloc_vfsmnt() but allocates the fake mnt_id for mount
+ * that is mounted or single cloned by ksu process
+ */
+static struct mount *susfs_alloc_non_unshare_ksu_vfsmnt(const char *name)
+{
+	struct mount *mnt = kmem_cache_zalloc(mnt_cache, GFP_KERNEL);
+	int res;
+
+	if (mnt) {
+		res = ida_alloc_min(&mnt_id_ida, DEFAULT_KSU_MNT_ID, GFP_KERNEL);
+		if (res < 0)
+			goto out_free_cache;
+
+		mnt->mnt_id = res;
+
+		if (name) {
+			mnt->mnt_devname = kstrdup_const(name,
+							 GFP_KERNEL_ACCOUNT);
+			if (!mnt->mnt_devname)
+				goto out_free_id;
+		}
+
+#ifdef CONFIG_SMP
+		mnt->mnt_pcp = alloc_percpu(struct mnt_pcp);
+		if (!mnt->mnt_pcp)
+			goto out_free_devname;
+
+		this_cpu_add(mnt->mnt_pcp->mnt_count, 1);
+#else
+		mnt->mnt_count = 1;
+		mnt->mnt_writers = 0;
+#endif
+
+		INIT_HLIST_NODE(&mnt->mnt_hash);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_child);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_mounts);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_list);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_expire);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_share);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_slave_list);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_slave);
+		INIT_HLIST_NODE(&mnt->mnt_mp_list);
+		INIT_LIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_umounting);
+		INIT_HLIST_HEAD(&mnt->mnt_stuck_children);
+		mnt->mnt.mnt_userns = &init_user_ns;
+	}
+	return mnt;
+
+#ifdef CONFIG_SMP
+out_free_devname:
+	kfree_const(mnt->mnt_devname);
+#endif
+out_free_id:
+	mnt_free_id(mnt);
+out_free_cache:
+	kmem_cache_free(mnt_cache, mnt);
+	return NULL;
+}

+#ifdef CONFIG_KSU_SUSFS
+/* - To retrieve the non sus mnt_id from mount */
+int susfs_get_non_sus_mnt_id_from_mnt(struct mount *orig_mnt) {
+	struct mount *mnt = orig_mnt;
+	int mnt_id;
+
+	if (mnt->mnt_id < DEFAULT_KSU_MNT_ID)
+		return mnt->mnt_id;
+
+	lock_mount_hash();
+	for (; mnt && mnt->mnt_parent && mnt != mnt->mnt_parent && mnt->mnt_id >= DEFAULT_KSU_MNT_ID; mnt = mnt->mnt_parent) { }
+	mnt_id = mnt->mnt_id;
+	unlock_mount_hash();
+	return mnt_id;
+}
+
+/* - To retrieve the non sus vfsmount from vfsmount, takes a reference on &mnt->mnt and mnt->mnt.mnt_root */
+struct vfsmount *susfs_get_non_sus_vfsmnt_from_vfsmnt(struct vfsmount *vfsmnt) {
+	struct mount *mnt = real_mount(vfsmnt);
+
+	if (mnt->mnt_id < DEFAULT_KSU_MNT_ID) {
+		mntget(&mnt->mnt);
+		dget(mnt->mnt.mnt_root);
+		return &mnt->mnt;
+	}
+
+	lock_mount_hash();
+	for (; mnt && mnt->mnt_parent && mnt != mnt->mnt_parent && mnt->mnt_id >= DEFAULT_KSU_MNT_ID; mnt = mnt->mnt_parent) { }
+	mntget(&mnt->mnt);
+	if (!mnt->mnt.mnt_root || IS_ERR(mnt->mnt.mnt_root)) {
+		mntput(&mnt->mnt);
+		unlock_mount_hash();
+		return vfsmnt;
+	}
+	dget(mnt->mnt.mnt_root);
+	unlock_mount_hash();
+	return &mnt->mnt;
+}

+#ifdef CONFIG_KSU_SUSFS_SUS_MOUNT
+static int susfs_show_vfsmnt(struct seq_file *m, struct vfsmount *mnt)
+{
+	struct proc_mounts *p = m->private;
+	struct mount *r = real_mount(mnt);
+	struct path mnt_path = { .dentry = mnt->mnt_root, .mnt = mnt };
+	struct super_block *sb = mnt_path.dentry->d_sb;
+	int err;
+
+	if (r->mnt_id >= DEFAULT_KSU_MNT_ID)
+		return 0;
+
+	if (sb->s_op->show_devname) {
+		err = sb->s_op->show_devname(m, mnt_path.dentry);
+		if (err)
+			goto out;
+	} else {
+		mangle(m, r->mnt_devname ? r->mnt_devname : "none");
+	}
+	seq_putc(m, ' ');
+	/* mountpoints outside of chroot jail will give SEQ_SKIP on this */
+	err = seq_path_root(m, &mnt_path, &p->root, " \t\n\\");
+	if (err)
+		goto out;
+	seq_putc(m, ' ');
+	show_type(m, sb);
+	seq_puts(m, __mnt_is_readonly(mnt) ? " ro" : " rw");
+	err = show_sb_opts(m, sb);
+	if (err)
+		goto out;
+	show_mnt_opts(m, mnt);
+	if (sb->s_op->show_options)
+		err = sb->s_op->show_options(m, mnt_path.dentry);
+	seq_puts(m, " 0 0\n");
+out:
+	return err;
+}
+
+static int susfs_show_mountinfo(struct seq_file *m, struct vfsmount *mnt)
+{
+	struct proc_mounts *p = m->private;
+	struct mount *r = real_mount(mnt);
+	struct super_block *sb = mnt->mnt_sb;
+	struct path mnt_path = { .dentry = mnt->mnt_root, .mnt = mnt };
+	int err;
+
+	if (r->mnt_id >= DEFAULT_KSU_MNT_ID)
+		return 0;
+
+	seq_printf(m, "%i %i %u:%u ", r->mnt_id, r->mnt_parent->mnt_id,
+		   MAJOR(sb->s_dev), MINOR(sb->s_dev));
+	if (sb->s_op->show_path) {
+		err = sb->s_op->show_path(m, mnt->mnt_root);
+		if (err)
+			goto out;
+	} else {
+		seq_dentry(m, mnt->mnt_root, " \t\n\\");
+	}
+	seq_putc(m, ' ');
+
+	/* mountpoints outside of chroot jail will give SEQ_SKIP on this */
+	err = seq_path_root(m, &mnt_path, &p->root, " \t\n\\");
+	if (err)
+		goto out;
+
+	seq_puts(m, mnt->mnt_flags & MNT_READONLY ? " ro" : " rw");
+	show_mnt_opts(m, mnt);
+
+	/* Tagged fields ("foo:X" or "bar") */
+	if (IS_MNT_SHARED(r))
+		seq_printf(m, " shared:%i", r->mnt_group_id);
+	if (IS_MNT_SLAVE(r)) {
+		int master = r->mnt_master->mnt_group_id;
+		int dom = get_dominating_id(r, &p->root);
+		seq_printf(m, " master:%i", master);
+		if (dom && dom != master)
+			seq_printf(m, " propagate_from:%i", dom);
+	}
+	if (IS_MNT_UNBINDABLE(r))
+		seq_puts(m, " unbindable");
+
+	/* Filesystem specific data */
+	seq_puts(m, " - ");
+	show_type(m, sb);
+	seq_putc(m, ' ');
+	if (sb->s_op->show_devname) {
+		err = sb->s_op->show_devname(m, mnt->mnt_root);
+		if (err)
+			goto out;
+	} else {
+		mangle(m, r->mnt_devname ? r->mnt_devname : "none");
+	}
+	seq_puts(m, sb_rdonly(sb) ? " ro" : " rw");
+	err = show_sb_opts(m, sb);
+	if (err)
+		goto out;
+	if (sb->s_op->show_options)
+		err = sb->s_op->show_options(m, mnt->mnt_root);
+	seq_putc(m, '\n');
+out:
+	return err;
+}
+
+static int susfs_show_vfsstat(struct seq_file *m, struct vfsmount *mnt)
+{
+	struct proc_mounts *p = m->private;
+	struct mount *r = real_mount(mnt);
+	struct path mnt_path = { .dentry = mnt->mnt_root, .mnt = mnt };
+	struct super_block *sb = mnt_path.dentry->d_sb;
+	int err;
+
+	if (r->mnt_id >= DEFAULT_KSU_MNT_ID)
+		return 0;
+
+	/* device */
+	if (sb->s_op->show_devname) {
+		seq_puts(m, "device ");
+		err = sb->s_op->show_devname(m, mnt_path.dentry);
+		if (err)
+			goto out;
+	} else {
+		if (r->mnt_devname) {
+			seq_puts(m, "device ");
+			mangle(m, r->mnt_devname);
+		} else
+			seq_puts(m, "no device");
+	}
+
+	/* mount point */
+	seq_puts(m, " mounted on ");
+	/* mountpoints outside of chroot jail will give SEQ_SKIP on this */
+	err = seq_path_root(m, &mnt_path, &p->root, " \t\n\\");
+	if (err)
+		goto out;
+	seq_putc(m, ' ');
+
+	/* file system type */
+	seq_puts(m, "with fstype ");
+	show_type(m, sb);
+
+	/* optional statistics */
+	if (sb->s_op->show_stats) {
+		seq_putc(m, ' ');
+		err = sb->s_op->show_stats(m, mnt_path.dentry);
+	}
+
+	seq_putc(m, '\n');
+out:
+	return err;
+}
+#endif // #ifdef CONFIG_KSU_SUSFS_SUS_MOUNT
+

 {

+	}

 }

+static int susfs_statfs_by_dentry(struct dentry *dentry, struct vfsmount *mnt, struct kstatfs *buf, bool *is_fuse)
+{
+	int retval;
+
+	if (!dentry->d_sb->s_op->statfs)
+		return -ENOSYS;
+
+	memset(buf, 0, sizeof(*buf));
+	retval = security_sb_statfs(dentry);
+	if (retval)
+		return retval;
+	if (!susfs_sus_kstat_spoof_vfs_statfs(d_backing_inode(dentry), buf, is_fuse)) {
+		if (buf->f_frsize == 0)
+			buf->f_frsize = buf->f_bsize;
+		return retval;
+	}
+	retval = dentry->d_sb->s_op->statfs(dentry, buf);
+	if (retval == 0) {
+		buf->f_flags = calculate_f_flags(mnt);
+		if (buf->f_frsize == 0)
+			buf->f_frsize = buf->f_bsize;
+	}
+	return retval;
+}
+#endif // #ifdef CONFIG_KSU_SUSFS_SUS_KSTAT
